# DSP workbench

A browser-based modular DSP lab: build signal chains by wiring nodes on a canvas. Runs fully client-side, no build step, works offline as a PWA.

**Live demo: [fagci.github.io/dsp](https://fagci.github.io/dsp/)**

[![FT8 propagation map in DSP workbench — open the demo](docs/screenshots/map-ft8.png)](https://fagci.github.io/dsp/)

[More screenshots ↓](#screenshots)

## Features

### Workbench
- Node graph editor: pan/zoom canvas, drag-and-drop modules, typed ports (signal, number, spectrum, image, text, block, IQ stream)
- Dashboard view (split panes, draggable dividers) for building instrument-like UIs
- **Tiles-only mode**: open the app with `?readonly-tiles` (e.g. `index.html?readonly-tiles#p=My patch`) — only the tiles are shown, no sidebar, toolbar, pane headers or graph; layout and graph cannot be edited or moved, hotkeys are off, the mode is not saved. Pressing any button on a source module while the engine is stopped starts it
  - As a standalone window, only the player: `google-chrome --app="https://fagci.github.io/dsp?readonly-tiles=1"` (pin the module to a tile first)

    ![Music Player in a Chrome app window, tiles-only mode](docs/screenshots/app-winamp.png)
- **Module graph** pane in the dashboard: the regular node canvas inside a tile, next to the modules opened in other panes (SunVox-style) — see [Module graph pane](#module-graph-pane)
- **Detail levels (LOD)**: double-tap a module's title to collapse it to a compact card (title only), to a **dot** (a circle with initials; the full name shows on hover / when selected) or back to full. Service modules (the auto adapters *IQ → Audio*, *I/Q → IQ*) are added as dots. The **detail** button in the toolbar switches all modules at once: full → compact → dots. The level is saved in patches
- **Power profile** (⚡ in the toolbar): CPU share of every module (processing + drawing) as a badge on the module and a top-10 list, the total, and battery drain (%/h, where `navigator.getBattery` exists). Click a row to freeze a module. **skip unused** (on by default) does not compute modules whose result nobody uses: no display and no wire to a used module (outputs, sources and control modules are always kept)
- **Quick chain** (**→+** in the toolbar, enabled when one module is selected): the picker opens for the module's free signal output (a module without one — its free signal input, e.g. Sound Card), lists only what fits, connects the new module and selects it, so the next tap continues the chain — a receiver or a patch can be built with taps alone, without dragging wires
- **Module picker**: search takes several words (all must match title / id / category; titles starting with a word rank first). Drop a wire on an empty spot — or select a port and tap an empty spot — and the picker lists only modules that have a matching port, shows which pin the wire will go to and connects it on pick; modules that need an automatic *iq* ↔ signal adapter come after the exact matches. Every module gets a generated icon: initials on the category colour, the shape follows the main output type (circle — IQ, diamond — spectrum, square — records / text / images, hexagon — no outputs)
- **Wire bundles**: wires are bundled by topology, not by geometry. A module with two or more wires on one side gathers them into one harness at that side — they fan out at the pins, lie 3 px apart along the trunk and part only near their targets; wires from one module to different modules share the trunk, wires from different modules into one module converge before it. Each wire stays separate (its own colour, hover, click to delete). Automatic and independent of where the targets are: modules stacked vertically, close together or behind (a wire going backwards) are bundled as well. The toolbar button (wires icon) turns it off; the choice is remembered
- Add modules right on the canvas: double-click an empty spot (or **+** in the graph pane) and search
- Groups (nested subgraphs) with custom inputs/outputs
- Undo/redo, duplicate, multi-select, module search — from the toolbar; hotkeys: **Delete** (removes the selected modules) and **Ctrl+Z** / **Ctrl+Shift+Z** (undo / redo). Patches from the side list can be **dragged onto the canvas** (or inserted with **＋**) — they are added to the current graph, not replacing it, and one Ctrl+Z takes them back
- Save/load patches to local storage or JSON files
- 160+ built-in presets: demos, quick scenarios, radio protocols, music, analysis
- Adjustable block size, sample rate and run speed (×1…×32); redraw rate switch in the settings panel (the sliders button in the toolbar, together with run speed, block size, sample rate, **All data → Export… / Import…** and the global **CORS proxy** used by Satellites, Horizon and Station Schedule — `{url}` in it is replaced by the encoded address, otherwise the address is appended): *fps max* (default; always 60 fps — the waterfall does not slow down at rest, but the main thread has less time for USB and audio), *fps auto* (60 → 30 → 20 fps as the interface sits idle, 10 fps with a USB SDR connected) and *fps min* (10 fps, lightest on CPU)
- AudioWorklet engine, SharedArrayBuffer path when cross-origin isolated
- Installable PWA with offline support; app badge while the engine runs; autosave runs in idle time under a Web Lock (no double saves from two tabs); `prefers-reduced-motion` is respected
- **Offline data** (`OfflineData` in `modules/filestore.js`): big files that modules download once — the Object Detect model and TF.js (~19 MB, `vendor/objdet/`), the JS8 text dictionary, the Natural Earth basemap — are kept in *Files → Offline data* (see the size, delete, export, or put your own file with the same name). Items marked `auto` (the JS8 dictionary and the basemap) are also fetched quietly ~20 s after start when online (not on cellular, not in data-saver mode); Object Detect loads on first use. The *⇩ offline* button in Files lists everything worth having without a network (Object Detect model, JS8 dictionary, basemap, cities, satellite TLE and SatNOGS transmitters) with a ✓ for what is already stored, and downloads the selected items; every item also has a **by hand** box for when the network is blocked: links to the sources (opened in a new tab; the app's own files such as the basemap and the JS8 dictionary are saved with a click) and *choose file…* to put the downloaded file back — models and libraries go to Files → Offline data under the right name, cities (GeoNames JSON / dump / CSV), SatNOGS transmitters (JSON) and each CelesTrak TLE group are checked and stored in their own storage (a wrong file is refused); modules add their own with `OfflineData.fileItem` / `OfflineData.item` (an `item` gives `manual()` links and `ingest(file, entry)`). They are not synced between devices and are not duplicated in the service-worker cache.
- Large patches stay smooth: nodes outside the screen are not laid out or painted (`content-visibility: auto`, enabled per node after its first layout so the size is remembered; on a 128-node test patch 4–7 → 28–54 fps)
- Built-in performance counters (long tasks/frames, slow input events, `Perf.time` marks, device profile) are appended to the error report (`core-perf.js`)

### Sources
- Oscillator, sweep/jammer, constant, LFO (a `sync` input restarts the phase on a rising edge, the `sync` output pulses at the start of every period — chain it with the oscillator's `sync` or another LFO), text source
- Microphone (stereo A+B), audio file, audio stream URL, tab/screen audio capture
- **Not from this reality** (synthetic, nothing is received): *Strange Attractor* (Lorenz / Rössler / Thomas, three outputs — try the phase scope), *Black Hole Merger* (inspiral chirp → ringdown → silence, with a `sync` pulse), *Pulsar* (period, flicker, missing pulses, scatter tail), *Schumann Resonance* (five Earth–ionosphere modes driven by lightning, a frequency multiplier brings it into the audible range), *Cellular Automaton* (Wolfram rules, rule 30 as a bit generator)
  - These are teaching models, not physics simulations. *Strange Attractor* uses the standard parameters (the largest Lyapunov exponent measured: Lorenz 0.91, Rössler 0.07). *Black Hole Merger* is the Newtonian inspiral chirp (f ∝ (t_c − t)^-3/8, A ∝ f^2/3) with a simple damped ringdown at 1.2 × the merger frequency, the duration is stretched to be audible, the noise is white. *Pulsar* has correlated flicker and a scatter tail (convolution with an exponential), but no dispersion. *Schumann Resonance* has real mode frequencies, the relative amplitudes are set by hand
- **USB SDRs** directly via WebUSB: RTL-SDR, HackRF, Airspy R2/Mini, Airspy HF+, HydraSDR RFOne, SDRplay RSP1 / MSi2500, RX-888 (mkI/mkII/mkIII) — multiple tuners/demodulators per device, wideband sweep with a panoramic waterfall, IQ recording and playback in WAV / SigMF (see [USB SDR](#usb-sdr))
- **KiwiSDR** remote receivers (public list included); **SDR Receivers Directory** (Geo) puts public KiwiSDR / SpyServer receivers on the Map — click a point and its host:port goes to the KiwiSDR node (see [KiwiSDR behind HTTPS](#kiwisdr-behind-https))
- **OpenWebRX** / OpenWebRX+ remote receivers: the *OpenWebRX* node connects to `ws://host:port/ws/`, the server demodulates (AM / LSB / USB / CW / NFM / WFM), the node outputs `audio`, `rssi` and `spec` — the server's own band spectrum (FFT frames, float32 or ADPCM) on an absolute frequency axis, so it can go straight to Spectrum Analyzer. Wired the same way as **USB SDR**: `spec` → analyzer `spec`, analyzer `f1` → `tuneFreq`; the analyzer draws the channel curtain (passband, mode, bandwidth) around the tuned frequency, shows the passband under the cursor, and dragging a curtain edge sets `bw` (analog modes). `tuneFreq`, `bw` and `demod` (USB SDR names — USB, LSB, AM, NFM, WFM…) are inputs, `tuneFreq`, `bw`, `freqLo`, `freqHi` outputs; `steerFreq` is accepted and ignored (the server's band is chosen with *profile*) **Profiles** (the server's bands / receivers): the *profile* parameter (or the `profile` input — a name, id or part of a name) switches them; a switch changes the receiver for all listeners of the server, so it is never done on its own — out of the band the status lists the other profiles. **Modes** come from the server (*mode* list, only the ones its features allow); digital modes (FT8, WSPR, JS8, APRS, POCSAG, DMR, YSF, D-STAR, NXDN…) are decoded by the server: the node sets the underlying modulation (*under*, default the server's first) and the secondary mode, decoded messages and metadata go to `rec` (`kind` = `decoded` / `metadata`, `t`, the server's fields) and the last one as a line to `text`; `profile` outputs the current profile name. The delay is set by *audio buffer* (default 300 ms: that much is collected before playback and after an underrun; a backlog above three buffers is dropped) The frequency is absolute (the offset from the server's band centre is computed by the node; outside the band the status says which range the server covers); plain and IMA-ADPCM audio are supported. Same proxy rules as for KiwiSDR. Checked against the protocol of the reference client's sources and a fake server in `tools/test-openwebrx.mjs`, **not against a live server**; profile switching is not used
- Camera, video, image, accelerometer and Generic Sensor API (*Camera*: **find all cameras** lists every video input the browser exposes — extra lenses, IR / night-vision, USB thermal cameras that show up as UVC — and lets you pick one; what the browser does not expose stays invisible)
- **Motion Detect**, **Pulse (camera)**, **Timelapse**, **Color / Spectrometer** (Video): what to do with a camera picture besides looking at it — see [Camera tools](#camera-tools)
- **Barcode / QR** (Video): reads QR codes and barcodes from a camera, video or image frame (BarcodeDetector — Chrome / Android; where the browser has none, QR codes are read by the built-in jsQR, barcodes of other kinds need BarcodeDetector): the text, a pulse on `go`, records `{format, value}`, a repeat of the same code is ignored for a set time
- **Object Detect** (Video, `modules/objdet.js`, logic in `modules/objdet-kernels.js`, `node tools/test-objdet.mjs`): finds objects (80 COCO classes: person, car, dog, cat, phone…) in a photo or video. Inputs: `vid` (live video — Camera, Video) and `img` (photo, IP Camera frames); a photo is detected once (again when a setting changes), video every *detect every* seconds. Outputs: `img` (the frame with boxes; read from the canvas only when something is wired to it), `text` (`#id label 87%` lines), `go` (pulse), `rec` (`{label, score, x, y, w, h[, id]}`, fractions of the frame), `count`, `total`. Parameters: min confidence, only these classes (`person, car`), pause between detections (counted from the end of the previous one, default 0.5 s; nothing runs while the tab is hidden), detection size (the net shrinks the frame to ~300 px anyway; default 320), picture width, advanced *compute* (auto / webgl / cpu: if the browser crashes with SIGILL on a device, try cpu — slower, but it does not touch the GPU driver), **tracking** (greedy IoU matching per class: every object keeps an id, a lost one is remembered for *keep* seconds; with tracking `go`/`rec` fire only for **new** objects, `total` counts them — people counter, door watcher). COCO-SSD on TensorFlow.js; TF.js and the model (about 7 MB) load from jsDelivr and Google storage on the first start, so the first run needs a network. For offline use put `tf.es2017.min.js` (not `tf.min.js`: it needs `unsafe-eval`, which the page CSP forbids), `coco-ssd.min.js` and the `model.json` with its shards into `vendor/` and set the advanced *script URLs* and *model.json URL*. Preset **Object Detect: Camera Video and Photo**. Checked in headless Chromium with the real TF.js and model; not tried on a real camera.
- **QR Code** (Video, `modules/qr.js`, pixels in `modules/qr-kernels.js`, `node tools/test-qr.mjs`): text (the `text` input or the parameter, UTF-8) → a QR picture on `img` (error correction L / M / Q / H, pixels per module). The generator (`vendor/qrcode.min.js`, MIT) and the reader (`vendor/jsqr.min.js`, Apache-2.0, licences in `vendor/qr-LICENSE`) load on first use and work offline. Sync devices has **Show QR** (the pair key as a link) and **Scan QR** (camera, the key is taken from the link or from plain text), so a key no longer has to be typed or sent. Preset **QR: Generator and Reader Loop (no hardware)**.
- **WebSerial on Android**: Chrome for Android has no `navigator.serial`, so `modules/webusb-serial.js` provides it over WebUSB (OTG cable): CDC-ACM (tinySA, Flipper, Pico, native-USB ESP32), CH340/CH341 and CP210x adapters. Not tried on real hardware
- **tinySA / tinySA Ultra** spectrum analyzer over WebSerial: sweep into the spectrum/waterfall, screenshots, signal generator (see [tinySA](#tinysa))
- **ESP-SDR (ESP32)** over WebSerial: raw I/Q bursts from an ESP32's built-in radio ([ESPARGOS/esp-sdr](https://github.com/ESPARGOS/esp-sdr) firmware), spectrum in the browser (see [ESP-SDR](#esp-sdr))
- Serial port (WebSerial): reads lines from the device and now sends text to it too — a `text` wire or the Send field (each line goes with `\n`; `\\n`, `\\t`, `\\xHH` escapes), e.g. commands to firmwares like [`tools/radio-bridge-esp32c5`](tools/radio-bridge-esp32c5/radio-bridge-esp32c5.ino) (ESP32-C5 Wi-Fi/BLE/Zigbee USB bridge: `MODE WIFI|BLE|ZB`, `CH`, `TX …`), **Table** (one node for lists, band plans, bookmarks, logs and the data sequencer: CSV / TSV / JSON / TXT / KML / GPX / GeoJSON, folders in the browser DB; the rows are played one by one by [Row Player](#row-player)), Trigger Clock, Time Base — see [Table](#table)
- **Bluetooth LE** (Web Bluetooth): **BLE UART** (Nordic UART, HM-10 / FFE0 or your own UUIDs — a serial terminal without a cable), **BLE GATT** (any characteristic: notifications or periodic read, write; formats — heart rate, battery, temperature, uint / int / float, hex), **BLE Advertisements** (RSSI, TX power and manufacturer data of one device without connecting — proximity, finding a beacon) — see [Bluetooth LE](#bluetooth-le)
- **IQ over Network**: a remote SDR as an `iq` source — **rtl_tcp** through a TCP → WebSocket bridge (header, rate, tuning, gain, ppm, bias-T, direct sampling are sent as rtl_tcp commands; a wire on the frequency retunes) or a **raw stream** (uint8 / int8 / int16 / float32, rate and center set by hand) from any program that writes IQ to a pipe, or **SpyServer** through the same bridge — see [Remote SDR](#remote-sdr)
- **WebRTC Data**: a direct browser-to-browser channel (RTCDataChannel) with no server — the connection code (offer / answer) is passed by hand, through a messenger or the clipboard: *Create offer* on one side, *Accept offer* on the other, *Apply answer* back. Then text goes like **Text over Network** (lines, JSON into records) and the `send` input writes to the other side. Same network needs no STUN, over the internet set a STUN server
- **Gamepad** (Gamepad API): a gamepad, joystick, steering wheel or pedals as numbers — axes `a1…` (with a dead zone) and buttons `b1…` (analog triggers 0…1), the counts are parameters, the device is chosen by index or the first connected one; the `rumble` / `weak` inputs vibrate it. The browser shows the device only after a button is pressed on it
- **Joystick** (Sources): two sticks and eight buttons on the screen and on the keyboard — click the pad, then **W A S D** is the left stick, the **arrows** the right one, **Space, Enter, Z, X, Q, E, R, F** are buttons `b1…b8`; on a phone drag the sticks with your fingers (multi-touch) and press the buttons. Outputs: `x1 y1 x2 y2` (−1…1, with a dead zone and expo), `b1…b8`, `rec` and a ready **frame** as `text` by a template: `{x1}` −1…1, `{X1}` −100…100, `{r1}…{r4}` 1000…2000 µs (servos / RC), `{b1}…{b8}`, `{bits}`, `{hex}` (a 7-byte frame: `A5`, x1, y1, x2, y2 as signed bytes, buttons, XOR), `{n}` frame counter, `{t}` ms — wire it to *Serial Out*, *BLE UART*, *MQTT Out*, *Text over Network* or *nRF24* to drive a robot, a flight controller, a relay board or your own receiver. Frames go at *frames per second* while a key is held and once more as zeros on release (*keep-alive* repeats them at rest, a counter in the template lets the receiver notice a lost link). *hold*: a stick Y that keeps its value like a throttle. **Esc** is an emergency stop (all zeros until *Resume*). *invert vertical* turns the Y of the right, the left or both sticks over (for pitch: the down arrow / pulling back is up; the pad shows the result). The keyboard works while the pad is focused or always (not while typing in a field). A wire on `ex1…ey2` (for example from *Gamepad*) takes over that axis. Check a real machine without propellers first. Logic: `modules/control-kernels.js`, `node tools/test-control.mjs`
- **nRF24L01+** (Sources, over a USB bridge): an nRF24L01+ radio module on an Arduino Nano / Uno (or any board with the RF24 library) running [`tools/nrf24-bridge`](tools/nrf24-bridge/nrf24-bridge.ino) — the page talks to it over WebSerial (Chrome / Edge, 115200) with lines `CFG / TX / RXON / SCAN`. **Transmit**: text or hex from `send` goes out on the chosen channel (2400 + ch MHz), rate (250K / 1M / 2M), address (3–5 bytes), payload size (a fixed one pads the frame, 0 is dynamic), power, CRC and auto-acknowledge; `ok` shows whether the receiver answered. **Receive**: every packet as `rx` (text), `hex`, a pulse on `go` and a `rec` (channel, address, length, hex, text). **Scan**: the bridge listens for a carrier on all 126 channels several times and the share of hits comes out as `spec` (2400…2525 MHz) for the Spectrum Analyzer — to pick a channel clear of Wi-Fi. A wire on `ch` retunes. It talks to your own devices on your own channel and address and does not sniff other people's traffic. The firmware and the parser are checked against the protocol only (`node tools/test-control.mjs`, a simulated bridge in the browser), not on real hardware yet. nRF52 / nRF Connect boards over BLE: *BLE UART* (Nordic UART) and *BLE GATT*. Presets *Joystick: Keyboard and Screen Sticks → Serial*, *Joystick: Gamepad → Frame → MQTT*, *nRF24: Joystick → Radio Link*, *nRF24: Receive Frames*, *nRF24: 2.4 GHz Channel Scan*
- **Flight Sim** (Sources): an arcade aircraft on the *Joystick* or a gamepad, or a camera on an ADS-B aircraft; cameras chase / cockpit / tower / orbit for *Video Overlay* over *Horizon* terrain that is downloaded once and kept offline — see the node reference
- **HID Device** (WebHID): any USB / Bluetooth HID device without a driver — foot pedals, remote controls, USB scales and sensors, barcode scanners, your own boards (Leonardo / Pro Micro, RP2040). Input reports as hex and as records, one field of the report as a number (offset, format, bit, scale, report id filter), the `send` input writes output or feature reports. Keyboards and mice are closed to WebHID by the browser
- **FUNcube Dongle Pro+** (WebHID, `04d8:fb31`): tuner control — frequency (with ppm correction), LNA, mixer gain, IF gain 0–59 dB, bias-T, RF / IF filters; outputs `fc` for the `fc` input of **I/Q → IQ**. The IQ itself comes as USB audio: **Microphone** in *stereo device* mode at 192000 Hz, then **I/Q → IQ** (preset *FUNcube Dongle Pro+: Receiver*). The original FUNcube Dongle Pro (`fb56`) has a different command set and is not supported
- **Speech to Text** (Web Speech API — Chrome / Edge / Safari, the browser's own recognizer, listens to the default microphone, not a graph signal): every phrase as `text` and as a `rec` `{text, conf, lang}`, a pulse on `go`, interim text in the readout, language by a code (`ru-RU`)
- **NFC** (Web NFC, Chrome on Android over https): read NDEF tags — serial number, the first text / URL / JSON record as text, every record as a `rec`; write text, a URL or JSON to a tag (the *Write* button, a pulse on `go`, or a text on the `write` input)
- **MQTT In** (over WebSocket, QoS 0/1, username/password, auto-reconnect): subscribe to topic filters (`+` / `#`); a message comes out as text, topic, a number (the payload itself, or a JSON field — `temp.value`) and as records (a JSON object or array becomes `rec` with the topic added, so Tasmota / ESPHome / Home Assistant sensors with lat / lon go straight to the map). The browser cannot open `mqtt://` itself: give the broker a WebSocket listener (Mosquitto `listener 9001` + `protocol websockets`, EMQX, HiveMQ, the Home Assistant add-on); from the https page only `wss://` works — see [MQTT](#mqtt)
- **IP cameras**: *IP Camera* loads MJPEG / JPEG snapshot / HLS links from a Table over HTTP, *Camera Archive* records them by time or motion and plays them back on a timeline; fullscreen for any video module — see [IP cameras and archive](#ip-cameras-and-archive)
- **Podcasts**, **Webcams**, **Free Music** (no keys): podcast search and RSS feeds, OpenStreetMap webcams on the map with a refreshed picture, Creative Commons music from Openverse and the Internet Archive — see [Podcasts, Webcams, Free Music](#podcasts-webcams-free-music)
- **Bytes and files** (port `bin`): any file over any channel — **File → Bytes**, **Bytes → File**, **Bytes ↔ Text** (hex / base64 / UTF-8 / Latin-1), **Bytes → Image** (JPEG / PNG / GIF / WebP / BMP / AVIF, a JPEG stream such as MJPEG, raw pixels), **Bytes → Frames** / **Frames → Bytes** (numbered frames with CRC-32, carousel), and `bin` inputs / outputs on Serial Port, Serial Out, Text over Network (WebSocket binary), MQTT In / Out and BLE UART — see [Bytes and files](#bytes-and-files)
- **HEX Structure** (Data): a byte dump with the fields of a packet outlined, a field tree and editing — see [HEX Structure](#hex-structure). Structures are JSON (Ethernet, ARP, IPv4 / IPv6, UDP, TCP, ICMP, 802.11, ADS-B, DMR LC / CSBK / PI …), with byte and bit order, nested structures, lists, lengths and checksums; `bin` / `text` → `rec` and `rec` → `bin`, so a **Table** row becomes a packet and a decoded packet a row. **Wi-Fi Sniffer (ESP32)** feeds it 802.11 frames
- **Packet Link** (Sources): packets from WebSocket (a bridge to TCP / UDP / pcap), an HTTP stream, a serial port or BLE into records for Table, HEX Structure and PCAP Writer — see [Packet Link](#packet-link).
- **Termux (phone sensors)** (Sources, `modules/termux.js`, records in `modules/termux-kernels.js`, bridge [`tools/termux/bridge.mjs`](tools/termux/bridge.mjs), `node tools/test-termux.mjs`): an Android phone as a source — GPS, accelerometer, gyroscope, magnetometer, light, pressure, battery, Wi-Fi scan and cell towers. In Termux: `pkg install nodejs termux-api` (and the Termux:API app from F-Droid with location permission), then `node bridge.mjs` — a dependency-free WebSocket server on `127.0.0.1:8765`; the page on the same phone connects with the default URL. The node ticks the sources it needs and the bridge starts only those `termux-*` commands (nothing runs without a client; two clients get their own sets). Outputs: `rec` (`{src: gps|battery|sensor|wifi|cell, t, …}`; GPS records carry `id: 'me'`, Wi-Fi records carry `bssid`, `ssid`, `rssi`, `freq` and the last position, so they go straight into *Source Locator*), `line`, `go` and numbers `lat lon alt acc speed hdg`, `ax ay az`, `gx gy gz`, `mx my mz`, `light press`, `batt charging temp`. For another device: `node bridge.mjs --host 0.0.0.0 --token secret` and `ws://PHONE_IP:8765/?token=secret` (from the https page only `wss://` is allowed — use a TLS proxy or open the app over http). The bridge refuses WebSocket connections from other sites (`Origin` is checked: this app, localhost, `--origin URL` to add one), so a web page cannot read the phone's position behind your back. `--sources` sets what a plain *Text over Network* client gets, `--provider gps|network|passive`, `--sensor-delay` in ms. Preset *Termux: Phone Position, Sensors and Battery*. Checked with stub `termux-*` commands and a WebSocket client, not on a real phone yet
- **Text over Network**: WebSocket (`ws://`, `wss://`, with reconnect and a `send` input) HTTP(S) polling or an HTTP stream (SSE `text/event-stream` / NDJSON); lines one per block like the serial port, JSON objects/arrays straight into records. Example — Wi-Fi scan from Android (Termux): `websocat -t ws-l:0.0.0.0:8765 sh-c:'while :; do termux-wifi-scaninfo | jq -c .; sleep 30; done'`. [`tools/termux/wifi-scan.sh`](tools/termux/wifi-scan.sh) adds the phone's GPS position to every scan — preset *Wi-Fi: Locate Access Points (Termux)* puts each access point on the map while you walk around. From the https demo the browser only allows `wss://`/`https://` to other devices (`ws://`/`http://` work to localhost, or when the app is opened over http)

### Analysis
- Spectrum analyzer / waterfall (optional phosphor view; the waterfall keeps its history at full resolution, so zoom, dB range and palette changes redraw it without losing detail), persistence spectrum, oscilloscope (auto/normal/single trigger with level, slope, position, hysteresis and holdoff; sub-sample trigger alignment, averaging, persistence, sin(x)/x interpolation, XY, math channel, AC/DC coupling, time/level cursors, automatic measurements, Autoset, Run/Stop with history scroll), constellation, eye diagram
- CFAR signal detector (*trim edges* drops the sagging edges of a receiver window; the detector listens to the signals of a window **in turn** (by frequency; each until it ends or for *listen to each signal at most* ms): `listenF` is the one being heard (→ receiver `tuneFreq`), `todo` is how many are still to hear (→ Band Scanner `active`, so the scanner stays on the window until all are heard), `gate` is 1 while one is heard; **Blacklist to a Table:** the record also goes straight into a Table wired to `bl` / `fav` (no wait for the next engine block), and a Table shows a new row at once and writes it to the browser storage right after (a slow storage, e.g. behind a stream of log records on a phone, used to delay the row by seconds); `bl` emits a record `{freq, lo, hi, …}` for every blacklisted signal — wire it to the *Scan skip* Table that feeds `skip` and the list grows with the Table (editable, kept, shared; the presets and old patches are wired). *Clear blacklist* clears only the detector's own list, rows of the Table are removed in the Table. `gate` is high only while the heard signal is seen right now, so the receiver squelch (*squelch = input*, `gate` → `sqlGate`, short *squelch hang*) mutes quickly while the scanner still waits for the signal *hold* ms (`todo` keeps the window); the `plan` input takes the band plan: with *snap to the channel step* a signal is a channel of its range (`lo + k·step`) — one per channel, a carrier drifting inside it stays one target (`listenF` follows the carrier), and the result is the channel too — `f1…f4`, `listenF`, the `bands` labels, `rec` / `fav` / `bl` records and the blacklist carry the channel centre (the real carrier frequency stays in `freqReal`; the receiver channel is as wide as the mode, so a drift inside the channel is still heard); a signal *adjacent channel, dB* below a neighbour (±1 channel, a blacklisted one too) is dropped as its splatter; **Mark as interesting ★** (pin `favCur`, *Clear interesting* — `favClear`) puts the signal being heard on an interesting list: it is marked ★ on the spectrum, heard first in its window and for as long as it lasts (no per-signal limit), and a record `{kind:'interesting', freq, bw, db, snr}` goes to the `fav` output (a Table *Interesting* in the presets); every button (*Blacklist this signal*, *Skip signal in this window*, *Blacklist found signals*, *Clear blacklist*) has an input pin (`blCur`, `skipSig`, `blAdd`, `blClear`; a pulse 0→1). **Blacklist this signal** adds only the one being heard, **Skip signal in this window** moves on to the next one without blacklisting; *Blacklist found signals* puts what it sees now on a blacklist stored in the patch (at least one 12.5 kHz channel wide each, *Clear blacklist* wipes it; the `skip` input below is the same by ranges); the `bands` output carries the found signals (label, level) for the `sigs` input of the Spectrum Analyzer; the `skip` input takes the `bands` of a **Table** as a skip list — targets whose frequency falls inside a row (`lo`…`hi`, or a single `freq` with the target's width as tolerance) are ignored, so a scanner wired to `count` does not stop on permanent carriers; the `rec` output emits one record `{t, src:'CFAR', kind:'signal', id, freq, width, db, snr}` when a target is confirmed; `id` is the frequency rounded to *rec: same signal within* — a Table keeps every appearance, *Rec: Unique by Key* makes a list with count / first / last seen; noise estimate in linear power: OS — 75th percentile, robust to strong neighbours; SO — smallest of the two sides; CA — mean; a target is shown after M hits in the last N spectrum frames, so single noise spikes are dropped; outputs SNR of the strongest target and the noise floor), channel SNR, channel grid, **Scan Panel** (Radio) is a remote for the scan: a button acts at once on the Band Scanner / Signal Detector it is wired to (not through the 150 ms pulse, which a busy engine could miss; the pulse that follows on the wire is ignored as a duplicate), *Blacklist this signal* / *Mark interesting* use the last heard signal for 15 s when the current one has just been dropped (*listen to each signal at most*, a move to the next window); buttons *pause*, *Next window*, *Skip signal*, *Blacklist this signal*, *Mark interesting ★*, *Blacklist all found*, *Clear blacklist*, *Clear interesting* and the same as pulse outputs (150 ms) for the pins of the Band Scanner (`pause`, `next`) and the Signal Detector (`skipSig`, `blCur`, `favCur`, `blAdd`, `blClear`, `favClear`); inputs `freq` / `todo` / `idx` show what is heard now; the Multiband and Auto Scan presets wire it; band scanner (*pause* holds the current window, **Next window ▶** skips to the next one even while it listens to a signal; the `edge` output feeds the same trim value to the Spectrum Analyzer and the Signal Detector — set it once on the scanner; the readout shows the window number inside a range), auto frequency scanner (fixed sensitivity, or *over noise, dB*: the threshold follows a noise floor learned from the first 33 positions, so it does not depend on gain)
- **Antenna & Harmonics** (Radio): wavelength and dipole lengths (λ/4, λ/2, 5/8λ with a shortening factor) for a frequency, and the possible fundamentals f/k — a signal found while scanning may be the k-th harmonic of a transmitter on f/k, not the original. The *Spectrum Analyzer* shows the same on its markers (*markers: antenna length / harmonic source f/k*): λ/4 in the marker label and dashed ÷k ticks at f/k; if f/k is outside the view (a narrow SDR) it is shown as a badge at the edge with its frequency — tap it to retune the receiver there (needs the `centerFreq` output wired to the receiver)
- **Carrier tracking** in the *Spectrum Analyzer* (*markers: follow the carrier within bw*): every marker looks for the strongest peak within ±bw/2 around itself (bw is the width of the receiver channel under the marker, otherwise *carrier search bw*) and follows it, with sub-bin interpolation; the search window is shaded. A marker stays put when nothing is 6 dB above the noise floor, and markers driven by a wire (`m1`…`m4`) are not moved
- **Several wires into one input**: an input of type `rec` (records) or `bands` (band lists) takes any number of wires — records of all sources (decoders, scanners, a `rec` output of another node) come in as one list in the order the wires were drawn, so a map, a Rec Log or the *Spectrum Analyzer* needs no separate merge node (the old Rec Merge and Merge Band Plans nodes are gone; old patches are rewired on load). Other input types still take one wire. Pins of nodes with many of them: the main ones first, the rest under `+N`; the pair `I` / `Q` of a quadrature signal travels on one `iq` wire.
- **One node instead of several** (old patches convert on load): *Mixer* (2…12 channels, `channels` select) replaces Summer, Mixer 4 and Mixer 12; *Scale* (modes range / dB offset / linear scale, signal and number) replaces Signal Scale, Number Scale and Calibration; *Threshold* has a *window* mode (the former Value Window); *Hum Filter* has *adaptive* and *comb* modes (Hum Canceller and Comb Notch; the fixed Mains Notch is gone — the adaptive one does the same); *FFT* has a *low frequency* mode (Low-Frequency FFT); *Trigger Clock* has a *cron* mode; *Level* shows a bar (`view` = bar) and gives `db` (the old Level node); *Signal Type Identifier* replaces the crude Signal Recognition; the Auto Frequency Scanner is removed (Signal Detector, Frequency Peak and the band scanners cover it). Parameters that belong to one mode only are shown only in that mode.
- **Marks on the waterfall** in the *Spectrum Analyzer*: drag on the waterfall (with Alt, or always with *mark mode*) to frame a part of a signal and name it (*mark labels*: `preamble, data, voice`; type a number to pick one); click a mark to remove it; *time ticks* draw lines every 1…60 s. Marks are also set from the inputs **`marksTxt`** (text, one mark per line) and **`marksRec`** (records with the same fields): `from=433920 to=433950 color=purple label=start t=1231231123` or `f=433925 bw=12500 label="ctcss 75.5"`. `from`/`to` or `f`+`bw` give the band (`k`/`M` suffixes work; a bare number is Hz, or kHz / MHz when that falls inside the source span), without them the mark is a line across the whole width; `t` is epoch seconds (or ms), without it the mark is placed at once; `dur` (s) makes it taller; the time is shown only when there is no `label`. Marks live as long as the waterfall rows they sit on.
- Band plans and bookmarks ([Table](#table)), signal recognition
- **Multiband view** in the Spectrum Analyzer (*layout → panes*): wire a band list (a [Table](#table) with ticked lists, optionally filtered) to `bands` and every range gets its own pane side by side — a spectrum on top, a waterfall below, like on a tinySA. A Band Scanner (or a wide sweep) fills the panes as the receiver visits them; a pane that was not updated for a while is dimmed, the pane being received is outlined. *panes* — how many ranges at once (up to 8), *first band #* scrolls through a longer list; a click on a pane puts its centre on `centerFreq` (for a receiver's `steerFreq`); hover shows frequency and level; peak hold, palette and dB range work as usual. **Markers 1–4** work in panes too (tabs top right; a tap on a pane puts the active marker there, a tap on the pane title retunes the receiver on its centre) and their `f` / `db` / `snr` outputs read the data a pane has gathered, not just the window being received. Detection is a separate node: wire a **Signal Detector (CFAR)** to the same receiver spectrum (*auto range*, *trim edges* = the scanner's *edge*) and its `bands` to the analyzer's `sigs` — confirmed signals get a triangle and a frequency on the pane they fall into; the detector's `count` goes to the Band Scanner's `active` to stop it on a signal, `f1` to the receiver's `tuneFreq`, `rec` to a Table. Wide signals (broadcast FM) need larger guard / training bins, so it is meant for narrowband services. **View**: the wheel on a pane zooms (around the cursor), drag pans a zoomed pane, double click resets it; more ranges than *panes* are paged with ◀ ▶ (bottom right), the wheel on a pane title, or by themselves — *follow* turns the page after the Band Scanner (a manual page-turn pauses it for 10 s); every range keeps its own data while it is off screen. **Humps**: the edges of a receiver window droop, so a wide range stitched from windows shows a hump per window — *edge* (% of the window dropped on each side, 10 by default) on the analyzer and the same value on the Band Scanner (which then steps by the useful middle and starts the range with it) remove them. The edge of a window is still used where no other window has covered the range yet, so there are no gaps. The Band Scanner also learns the receiver's centre offset (*shift center off DC* and AM/SSB put the window `sr/4` above the requested frequency): it asks for `freq − offset`, so windows stand exactly on the range and narrow ranges are not missed. The preset keeps *fast retune* off and *settle* at 250 ms. The preset sets the receiver squelch to *input* (the `sqlGate` wire from the Band Scanner's `listening`): the speaker is silent while the scanner moves and opens only when it stands on a signal (or is parked on a marker); *squelch hang* keeps it open a little after. **Revisit rate**: the Band Scanner's *order* — *band by band* (the default) walks one range to its end before the next, so a wide range keeps the narrow ones waiting (a 16 MHz range takes ~9 windows, plus the listen timeout on every signal that is not skipped); *interleave* takes one window of every range in turn (`V L P V L P …`), so every pane is refreshed at the same pace and a wide range simply takes more rounds. **Scan non-stop until a marker**: *park the scanner on a marker* gives `holdF` — the frequency of the first marker that is set — which the Band Scanner takes on its `hold` input: it moves the window onto that frequency and stands there until the marker is cleared with its × — then the scan goes on from where it stopped. Without wiring `count` to `active` the scan is non-stop as well. **Gain by stages (RTL-SDR)**: USB SDR → advanced → *RTL gain mode*. *default* is the usual *auto* / *gain, dB* (LNA and mixer share one slider, VGA is fixed at step 8 = 16.3 dB); *stages* gives the R820T/R828D stages separately, as in r820tweak: *LNA step*, *mixer step* and *VGA step* (0–15 each; LNA and mixer can be left to the chip's own AGC with *LNA auto* / *mixer auto*). The readout shows `gain L9 M8 V8 ≈ 32.8 dB` in the same scale as the *gain, dB* slider (VGA step 8 adds nothing; another step adds or removes its difference). Handy against overload (the readout warns about it): lower LNA and mixer, keep VGA. Switching back to *default* restores the stage registers; with VGA step 8 the registers are identical to *gain, dB* of the same value. **Pattern that repeats with the window step**: the Band Scanner learns the receiver's centre offset from `freqLo` / `freqHi`; right after a retune those still show the previous window, and when the retune takes longer than *settle* a wrong offset was learned (e.g. −0.46 MHz) — the windows then jumped back and forth instead of stepping evenly, with spikes and humps at the seams. Now only the two real offsets are accepted (0 and `sr`/4 for *shift centre off DC*), anything else is treated as stale. Set *edge* on the scanner only: its `edge` output goes to the analyzer and the detector. The preset scans 144–176 and 400–470 MHz, *band by band*. **Pattern that repeats with the window step**: the Band Scanner learns the receiver's centre offset from `freqLo` / `freqHi`; right after a retune those still show the previous window, and when the retune takes longer than *settle* a wrong offset was learned (e.g. −0.46 MHz) — the windows then jumped back and forth instead of stepping evenly, with spikes and humps at the seams. Now only the two real offsets are accepted (0 and `sr`/4 for *shift centre off DC*), anything else is treated as stale. Set *edge* on the scanner only: its `edge` output goes to the analyzer and the detector. The preset scans 144–176 and 400–470 MHz, *band by band*. **dB range and gestures of a pane**: every pane has its own dB range (the *floor* / *top* of the analyzer are the default); the waterfall is recoloured with it. Vertical drag on a pane shifts its range, Ctrl+wheel (or a vertical two-finger pinch) scales it, the **⇕ dB** button (bottom row) fits every pane to its own data (noise −8 dB … peak +8 dB); a double click / double tap resets zoom and dB range of the pane. Two fingers: spreading along x zooms the frequency (the point under the fingers stays put), moving the fingers pans, spreading along y changes the dB range. A swipe along a pane title turns the page. The ranges are stored in the patch. The preset now uses *settle* 90 ms, *fresh frames* 2, *edge* 15 %. **Pattern with fast retune on**: with *fast retune* the PLL gets no waiting time and the first frames after a retune can carry a not yet settled frequency (the spectrum is shifted, and a wrong trace stays in peak hold). Now the receiver waits at least 15 ms before it counts a retune done, the panes drop the first frame after a window change, the scanner's *fresh frames* 0 now means the minimum of 2 (the first one is dropped by the panes, the second is recorded) — raise it to 3–4 only if the detector has to confirm signals, and the **⌫ peak** button (bottom row) clears the peak hold. The PLL lock bit read right after the register burst still showed the previous frequency, so a retune could count as done while the synthesizer was not locked: fast retune now waits 4 ms before the lock check and keeps the 8 kHz autotune step (≈7–8 transfers instead of ≈6; the final registers are the same as the full procedure). The wheel zoom of the panes is 5× slower. If the pattern is still there, switch *fast retune* off and keep *settle* ≥ 100 ms. **Why a scan can look stuck**: (1) the scanner is *parked* — a marker was set (a tap on a pane places one, a double click is two taps) while *park the scanner on a marker* is on: the panes now show a «⏸ scan parked on marker … » plate and a **▶ resume** button (clears the markers); the **⏸ park** button in the bottom row switches the parking on and off, and the taps of a double click (zoom reset) no longer leave a marker; (2) the Band Scanner's *loop* is off — one pass and it stops on the last window («done» in its readout); (3) *fresh frames* was 0 with the old patches: now 0 means *auto* — 3 frames when `spec` and `active` are wired, so the detector can confirm a signal (set 1–10 by hand, or unwire `spec` for a timer only); (4) the detector in the panes looks for narrow signals (guard 4 / training 32 bins): a wide plateau (a CDMA/LTE carrier, hundreds of kHz and more) is not marked. **Several receivers at once (up to 4)**: preset *USB SDR ×4: Multiband Scan (4 receivers)*. Every Band Scanner gets `lanes` = number of receivers and its own `lane` (0…lanes−1): the list of all windows (in the chosen *order*) is common and a lane takes every `lanes`-th window, so neighbouring windows are received at the same moment and a pass is ~`lanes`× shorter. The Spectrum Analyzer takes the receivers on `spec`, `spec2`, `spec3`, `spec4` and draws them in the same multiband panes; each receiver has its own Signal Detector (frames of different receivers do not break each other's confirmation) whose `count` / `f1` go to its scanner and to the receiver's `tuneFreq`; all `bands` outputs may go to the same `sigs`. A waterfall row of a wide range is finished when the windows of all receivers have covered it. Several identical dongles (the same serial) are fine: each USB SDR node takes a free one (a node reconnects to the same dongle only when the serials differ — `rtl_eeprom -s`). Use one sample rate; mind the USB hub (4 × 2.4 MS/s ≈ 19 MB/s). **Waterfall of the panes**: A waterfall row is now built strip by strip while the scanner passes the range, and the waterfall shifts down **only when the pass is over** (a range wider than a window: when the scanner comes back to the start; a range that fits into one window: one row per visit, and one row every *waterfall row interval* while the receiver stands on it). Note: with the Band Scanner's *loop* off the scan ends after one pass («done» in its readout) and the receiver stays on the last window. **Why a short *settle* did not work**: after a retune the USB queue (8 transfers × 25 ms) still returns ~200 ms of samples of the old frequency, which are dropped, and only then comes the spectrum of the new window; and the detector needs 2 frames of the new window to confirm a signal — a scanner that moves on after 60 ms sees no new frames at all (no waterfall rows, no detections). Two changes: while retunes are frequent (≥3 in 3 s) the receiver reads 5 ms chunks (200 reads/s instead of 40, the old-frequency backlog is ~40 ms; it goes back to normal when the scan stops), and the Band Scanner has a `spec` input and *fresh frames* (default 0 = timer only; the preset wires `spec` and uses 3): it decides only after that many spectrum frames **of the new window** (centre equal to the requested one), *settle* is then just a minimum wait (can be 0–60 ms). **Faster retuning (RTL-SDR)**: a tuning used to cost ~23 sequential USB control transfers (every tuner register was rewritten, even when it did not change). Now a register that already holds the value is skipped, and the PLL registers go in one burst — the same final register state with about half the transfers (≈12). The *fast retune* option (USB SDR → advanced; on in the Multiband Scan preset) goes further, like the `fast_retune` branch of rtl-sdr: no VCO fine-tune read, no switch of the PLL autotune to 8 kHz, no dummy read after the demodulator I2C switch — ≈5–6 transfers; the PLL lock is still checked and a failed lock falls back to the full procedure. After that the scanner's *settle* can be lowered (the receiver needs less time, the spectrum frames after a retune are the limit). Wide sweeps (*sweep*) get faster too. A pane title shows how long ago the range was last updated (after 3 s) and the scanner readout names the order in use — handy to see the revisit pace, and which version of the patch is running (an old saved patch keeps *band by band*; set *interleave* by hand). The scanner decides «a signal / move on» only after the receiver window actually stands on the requested centre, so frames of the previous window do not hold it for the listen timeout. A skipped signal is at least one channel wide (the range's `step`, 12.5 kHz without one), because a carrier drifts and a narrower skip loses it. **Search and skip**: a ▼ on a pane marks a signal of the Signal Detector (`sigs`), its `count` stops the scanner on it. A signal that should not stop it: *Blacklist found signals* on the detector, or a Table with `lo` / `hi` rows on its `skip` input (pagers, beacons). Preset **USB SDR: Multiband Scan (panes + CFAR)** wires it all: a range table → Band Scanner → SDR → panes, with a scan log **Quality**: *Averaging* smooths each pane's trace across passes (dB EMA), *Fade old data after, s* draws the parts the scanner has not visited for that long dim, the noise floor behind SNR is the lower 20% of a pane (a crowded range does not raise it), a pane takes 1024–4096 columns by the range step (no coarser than a channel), hover over the waterfall shows the age of the row, `⚠ N skipped` appears when rows are invalid or over 128; the **⤓ csv** button saves the peaks of all ranges (frequency, level, SNR, age), **⤓ png** the picture. The kernel is `modules/sa-panes-kernels.js` (`node tools/test-sapanes.mjs`).
- **Signal Analyzer** (Universal Radio Hacker style): captures IQ or a signal, demodulates it (ASK / OOK, FSK, PSK — found automatically), cuts it into messages and symbols, shows them on the plot and in a bin / hex / ascii table with labelled fields — see [Signal Analyzer](#signal-analyzer)
- **Blind analysis of an unknown transmission**: *Baud Estimator*, *CMA Equalizer*, *Sync Word Hunter*, *Conv Code Finder*, *CRC Finder* and a test transmitter *Unknown Signal* — from raw IQ to the message without knowing the symbol rate, the sync word, the code or the checksum (see [Blind analysis](#blind-analysis))
- **Signal type identifier**: finds every signal in a spectrum from any source and names its modulation — on an SDR from the raw IQ (see [Signal type identifier](#signal-type-identifier))
- Goertzel, autocorrelation, cross-correlation, frequency response / coherence
- Harmonics & THD, third-octaves, LUFS-like loudness, level statistics, spectral descriptors
- Impulse response & RT60, bird song analyzer, frequency meter, trend charts
- **Measuring instruments** ([details](#measuring-instruments)): *Modulation Meter* (AM depth, FM deviation, modulating frequency, FM index, carrier offset), *SINAD / THD+N*, *IQ Quality* (DC offset, I/Q gain and phase error, image rejection), *Signal Meter* (SNR, occupied bandwidth, bandwidth at −X dB, carrier frequency to fractions of a hertz), *Signal Database* (what a signal with this bandwidth, symbol rate, deviation and frequency can be), *PSK/QAM Receiver* (EVM, MER and carrier offset of BPSK / QPSK / 8PSK / 16QAM), *Channel Activity* (a log of channels with occupancy), *Cross-Correlator* (delay, phase, bearing and clock drift between two receivers)

### Processing
- Filters, gain, mixers (4/12 ch), AGC, squelch, mains notch, adaptive hum canceller (tracks mains frequency, all harmonics), comb notch, adaptive filter, spectral denoiser
- **Echo Canceller (AEC)** (Audio, `modules/aec.js`, logic in `modules/aec-kernels.js`, `node tools/test-aec.mjs`): removes from the microphone what the speaker played. Inputs `mic` and `ref` (what plays on the speaker / the far end, or a second microphone near a noise source): a partitioned-block frequency-domain adaptive filter (PBFDAF, overlap-save) learns the path ref → mic (delay and room tail up to 512 ms), converges in ~3 s (≈24 dB at 3 s, 50+ dB later on a synthetic path), keeps the near voice during double talk (adaptation slows when the error is loud; the first 2 s of an active reference adapt without that gate) and a soft suppressor removes the residual. Outputs `out`, `echo` (what was removed), `erle` (dB), `delay` (the path found, ms). **One microphone** (`ref` not wired): the filter predicts the signal from its own past later than *remove repeats later than* (20…400 ms) and subtracts it — echoes, a repeater that doubles the signal, long reverb; the voice itself stays (a sustained tone or a strongly periodic sound is predictable too and gets reduced, so keep the delay above the pitch period). Delay of the node: 256 samples (5.3 ms at 48 kHz); cost 3.5 % of a core for 128 ms, 7 % for 512 ms at 48 kHz (measured in Node).
- **Voice Restore (de-mumble)** (Audio, `modules/voicerestore.js`, logic in `modules/voicerestore-kernels.js`, `node tools/test-voicerestore.mjs`): for speech that went through a narrow filter and sounds muffled / mumbling / boomy. It learns from the speech itself (about 1 s of active speech): the long-term spectrum in ⅓-octave bands is compared with a typical speech spectrum (LTASS shape) and the bands are corrected (boost up to *max boost*, cut up to 12 dB; bands near the noise floor are not lifted), then the top that is missing above the found cut-off is rebuilt by shifting the lower octave up and shaping it like speech (the rebuilt top is plausible, not the original; on a synthetic test the spectral error of a 1.2 kHz wall dropped 20.6 → 12.3 dB with the equalizer and → 6.6 dB with the extension; speech that already has a typical spectrum changes by < 0.2 dB). Parameters: equalizer strength, extension, rumble cut (24 dB/oct below *boom*), max boost, learning time, bypass; output `cut` (the cut-off found, Hz). STFT with 75 % overlap, delay N samples (21 ms at 48 kHz). A mains hum or a drone: put the *Hum Filter* before it. Not a neural network: a blind equalizer plus bandwidth extension.
- **Neural audio** (`modules/nnaudio.js`, streaming wrapper in `modules/nnaudio-kernels.js`, `node tools/test-nnaudio.mjs`; models run in the browser, no network needed): **Denoiser (RNNoise)** (Audio) — a small recurrent network (112 KB WASM, `vendor/rnnoise.wasm`, Xiph RNNoise 0.1 built by Jitsi) that suppresses noise in speech; `in` → `out`, `mix` (0…1), outputs `vad` (speech probability) and `speech` (1 above the threshold). 10 ms delay; the net works on 48 kHz frames, other engine rates are resampled (linear). It needs a few seconds to learn stationary noise. Big models of this family are kept in Files → Offline data and listed under *⇩ offline*.
- **Sound Classifier (YAMNet)** (Analysis, `modules/yamnet.js`, logic in `modules/yamnet-kernels.js`, `node tools/test-yamnet.mjs`): Google YAMNet through MediaPipe Tasks Audio (WASM, in the browser) names the sound in a ~1 s window out of 521 AudioSet classes (speech, music, siren, engine, dog, white noise…). Input `in`; outputs `text` (top classes with shares), `label`, `score`, `hit` (1 while a class from *watch for* is heard — substring match, e.g. `speech` also catches «Male speech»), `rec` (an event when a watched class appears). Parameters: classify every, classes to show, min share, watch list, watch threshold. The window is averaged down to 16 kHz exactly (15 600 samples). ~11 MB (`vendor/mediapipe/`: MediaPipe 1.1.1 wasm + `yamnet.tflite`) are fetched once into Files → Offline data and listed under *⇩ offline*; SIMD build only.
- **Speech to Text (Whisper)** (Analysis, `modules/whisper.js`, logic in `modules/whisper-kernels.js`, worker `whisper-worker.js`, runtime in `vendor/whisper/`, `node tools/test-whisper.mjs`): OpenAI Whisper (ONNX, q8) through Transformers.js + ONNX Runtime Web (WASM, in a separate thread — the graph does not stall). Input `in`; the stream is cut into phrases by pauses (threshold in dBFS plus a self-adjusting noise floor, pause, longest phrase 5…28 s), each phrase is recognised: outputs `text` (last phrase), `full` (transcript), `go` (pulse per phrase), `rec` (event with text and time), `count`, `busy` (phrases waiting). ~100 languages (Russian included), *language* auto or fixed (fixed is more reliable on radio), *translate* to English; models tiny (≈45 MB) / base (≈85) / small (≈270) and their `.en` variants, or any ONNX Whisper repo from huggingface.co in *own model repo*. Typical Whisper inventions on silence («Thank you.», `[BLANK_AUDIO]`, loops) are dropped (*junk* option); optional `[m:ss]` stamps; buttons *Transcribe file…* (audio/video, faster than real time), *Clear*, *Save text*. The runtime (≈15 MB, `vendor/whisper/`) and the model files are fetched once into Files → Offline data (⇩ offline: runtime + tiny) and then work without a network; the worker never goes to the network itself — the page serves it the files. If huggingface.co is not reachable from the network, in Files → Offline data every item with http sources has a *by hand* section: open each link in a new tab (the browser saves the file), then *choose downloaded files…* and pick them — the names are matched (for Whisper `.onnx` go to `onnx/`) and the files land in Files → Offline data (`OfflineData.save`). The model shows up in the ticker via `rec`. See the preset «Speech to Text (Whisper)».
- **Notes (Basic Pitch)** (Analysis, `modules/pitchnn.js`, logic in `modules/pitchnn-kernels.js`, `node tools/test-pitchnn.mjs`): Spotify's Basic Pitch (TF.js, ~0.9 MB model in `vendor/basicpitch/`) hears several notes at once. Every *analyse every* s it takes the last ≈2 s, resamples to 22 050 Hz and reads the note probabilities of the last frames; outputs `text` (`C4 E4 G4`), `n`, `midi` and `hz` of the strongest, `rec` (an event when a note appears). The answer lags about a second; heavy on the CPU (≈2 s per window on the `cpu` backend, much less on WebGL). TF.js is shared with Object Detect; everything is fetched once into Files → Offline data (*⇩ offline*).
- FFT, Zoom-FFT (I/Q), Hilbert transform, quadrature shift, magnitude/phase, wavelet (constant-Q), cepstrum
- Beamformer, phase scope (X-Y), envelope, calibration, capture & loop
- Audio effects: delay, reverb, distortion, compressor/limiter, EQ, chorus/flanger/phaser, pitch shifter

### IQ blocks
- **IQ stream** wires carry complex (or real) samples at their own sample rate, not the engine's — build a receiver from blocks: source → frequency shift → decimator → demodulator → IQ → Audio (see [IQ blocks](#iq-blocks))
- USB SDR `iq` output (raw IQ at the native rate, also from IQ file playback), IQ generator, IQ spectrum, ADS-B demodulator, PSK (QPSK/OQPSK) demodulator

### Modulation & radio
- AM/FM/SSB demodulator, FSK demodulator, generic modulator
- Carrier acquisition, matched filter, symbol sync
- OFDM modulator/demodulator, chirp modem (transmit/receive)
- HF propagation, WWV/WWVH/CHU time decoder (100 Hz subcarrier: pulses of 200 / 500 / 800 ms classified at 350 / 650 ms; the bit layout is from memory, not checked against real reception) and **WWV: Test Signal** — the same code for a chosen minute plus the second ticks
- **Time Signal Decoder**: DCF77 (77.5 kHz) and WWVB (60 kHz) longwave time signals → bits → minute frame with parity / marker checks → UTC time (see [DCF77 and WWVB](#dcf77-and-wwvb))
- **SAME / EAS** (NOAA Weather Radio alerts, AFSK 520.83 Bd from an NFM receiver): ZCZC header with the three repeats voted → originator, event, FIPS areas, validity, station; a test-signal generator for the decoder (see [SAME / EAS](#same--eas))
- **SELCAL** (aviation selective calling, HF / VHF, ICAO 16 tones): the code of an aircraft call from audio — two pulses of two tones each → `AB-CD`, with the measured tuning error; a test-signal generator (see [SELCAL](#selcal))
- **Five-tone selcall** (land mobile: ZVEI-1/2/3, DZVEI, PZVEI, CCIR, EEA, EIA): sequences of 5 tones → the code with the repeat tone expanded; a test-signal generator (see [Five-tone selcall](#five-tone-selcall-zvei-ccir-eea))
- **TBSK** (spread-spectrum data over sound, [nyatla/TBSKmodem](https://github.com/nyatla/TBSKmodem)): a modulator (text → audio) and a decoder (audio → bytes / text) with a differential tone and delay detection, 5 bit/s … 1 kbit/s, no carrier lock (see [TBSK](#tbsk-spread-spectrum-data-over-sound))
- **RDS Display** (Radio): everything an FM station sends in RDS / RBDS on one screen — see [WFM: stereo and RDS](#usb-sdr)
- Doppler radar, 2D chirp radar, monostatic sonar

### Infrared
- **IR Encode / Decode / Learn** — NEC, NEC-ext, Samsung, Sony SIRC 12/15/20, RC5, RC6, JVC, Panasonic; unknown frames (air conditioners) are broken down into header, pulse lengths and bytes; any code can be stored and replayed. The nodes know nothing about the hardware, the adapters do: sound card (**IR Sound TX / RX**) and a serial port (**IR Serial**: Arduino / ESP / Pico, Flipper Zero) — see [Infrared](#infrared-1)

### Digital modes & decoders
- FT8 (receive — LSB audio (inverted tones, e.g. OpenWebRX in LSB) is detected by itself, *side* forces it; for LSB *dial* is the tuned frequency; **FT8: Transmit** packs a standard message — `CQ K1ABC FN42`, `K1ABC W9XYZ -12`, `R+05`, `RR73`, `73`, or free text up to 13 characters — into 79 GFSK tones and sends it at +0.5 s of every / even / odd 15 s UTC slot, so *FT8: Transmit → FT8: Receive Slots* is a loopback test), **JS8Call** (Normal mode, 15 s slots: *JS8Call: Receive Slots* decodes heartbeats, directed commands and text — LDPC(174,87) + CRC-12, records with call / addressee / locator / SNR like FT8; *JS8Call: Transmit* packs `HB [GRID]`, `CQ [DX|QRP|…]`, `W9XYZ SNR?`, `W9XYZ SNR -12` or `W9XYZ: free text` into 79 tones, one frame per slot; free text goes through the JSC dictionary, downloaded on first use from `data/js8-jsc.txt`; Fast / Turbo / Slow / Ultra and relayed `>` messages are not supported — see [JS8Call](#js8call)), RTTY, Morse (TX/RX, including from camera), DTMF, PSK31, Feld Hell
- Olivia, Contestia, AX.25/APRS (TX/RX)
- POCSAG pager transmitter (RIC, text / numeric / tone) → AM/FM/SSB Modulator → HackRF TX (see [POCSAG](#pocsag))
- WEFAX, NOAA APT, SSTV-style raster
- **Nipkow disk (mechanical television)**: **Nipkow Disk: Transmit** turns a picture (`img` / `vid`, or a built-in test pattern) into an audio scan signal — one line per hole of the disk, one turn = `spirals` fields — plus a sync pulse once per turn; **Nipkow Disk: Receive** builds the picture back. Disk variants (ready-made *disk* list, every field editable): Baird 30-line 12.5 fps, 24 / 48 / 50 lines, 2 and 3 spirals interlaced (a frame = k fields) or 2 pictures per turn, sweep from the outer or inner edge, either direction of rotation, window shape (sets the inner radius of the spiral), slow scan. Sync: *pulse* (separate wire), *in-band* (an extra hole before every field, below black; a longer mark flags the first field), *free* (manual period trim and shift) or *auto* (the turn period from autocorrelation — follows a drifting motor). **Colour**: red, green and blue pictures go out in turn (a filter wheel on the disk; 3 pictures or, interlaced, 3 frames per colour frame), the start of the cycle is marked by the sync pulse or by the long in-band mark; the receiver gives an RGB picture; a colour test pattern, camera and picture sources work. Geometry: *flat* (straight lines) or *disk* (the arcs the holes really draw — the picture is a sector); mixing them shows how a real televisor bent the picture. Presets: *Nipkow Disk: 30-Line Mechanical Television*, *…Interlaced Two-Spiral Disk, In-Band Sync, Noisy Line*, *…Receive from the Microphone*, *…Colour, Red-Green-Blue in Turn*; test: `node tools/test-nipkow.mjs`
- HFDL: full receive chain down to ACARS / ADS-C, aircraft tracks and ground stations on the map
- **ADS-B / Mode S** (1090 MHz) from raw SDR IQ: aircraft on the map with callsign, altitude, speed and heading (see [ADS-B](#ads-b))
- GSM: physical-layer receiver from raw IQ — FCCH tone sync, SCH decode (BSIC + frame number), BCCH System Information (cell ID, LAI, ARFCN lists, RACH, offline operator-name lookup), CBCH/Cell Broadcast text when it shares the received carrier, table-free blind decode of unencrypted SDCCH/SACCH signalling (CM Service Request, Identity, Authentication, Location Updating…), an optional uplink input for a lab/test stand, GMSK burst extraction (see [GSM](#gsm))
- **Meteor-M LRPT** (137 MHz) from raw IQ, frame level: QPSK/OQPSK demodulator, CCSDS decoder (Viterbi K=7, NRZ-M, ASM, derandomizer, RS(255,223)×4) → VCDU frames → **MSU-MR image** (packets, JPEG-like 8×8 segments, RGB composites, PNG) (see [Meteor-M LRPT](#meteor-m-lrpt))
- **Radiosondes: Vaisala RS41** (400–406 MHz) from raw IQ or FM audio: serial, position, height, climb, speed, temperature, humidity → map (see [Radiosondes](#radiosondes-rs41))
- **Inmarsat STD-C** (1.5 GHz): BPSK 1200 → frames → packets → SafetyNET / FleetNET EGC messages (see [Inmarsat STD-C](#inmarsat-std-c))
- **MPT 1327** (analogue trunking control channel): FM → FFSK 1200 Bd → codewords with CRC → PFIX/IDENT (see [MPT 1327](#mpt-1327))
- **ISM 433 MHz** (sensors, remotes, doorbells; OOK and FSK): pulse train → PWM / PPM / Manchester → EV1527/PT2262, Nexus, or an unknown packet with timings and bits (see [ISM 433](#ism-433))
- **LoRa** (chirp spread spectrum, SF7–12, 7.8–500 kHz): decoder (preamble, sync word, SFD, header, Hamming, interleaving, CRC; measures SNR and carrier offset) and modulator (text or hex → IQ, also to HackRF TX) (see [LoRa](#lora))
- **AIS** (marine VHF, 161.975 / 162.025 MHz): GMSK 9600 Bd → HDLC with CRC → position reports, static data, base stations and aids to navigation; vessels on the map with name, type and size, `!AIVDM` sentences on a text output (see [AIS](#ais))
- **POCSAG** pagers (512 / 1200 / 2400 Bd, 2-FSK): FM → BCH(31,21) codewords with error correction → RIC, function, text and numeric messages, tone-only calls; to the Messages node (see [POCSAG](#pocsag))
- **ACARS** (VHF 131 MHz, AM + MSK 2400 Bd): envelope → MSK → blocks with CRC and parity → aircraft registration, label, flight, text (see [ACARS](#acars))
- **Digital Voice Decoder** — one decoder for **DMR, P25 Phase 1, NXDN 9600 and 4800, YSF, M17, D-STAR and dPMR**: the protocol is found by its sync words (or picked by hand), records and raw vocoder frames on separate outputs, turned into sound by the mbelib [Vocoder](#vocoder-mbelib) (see [4FSK Digital Voice](#digital-voice-decoder-4fsk))
- **TETRA** (base-station downlink, π/4-DQPSK 18 kBd, 25 kHz channels): sync burst → cell (MCC / MNC, colour code, frequency, location area, services), slot timing, AACH, calls by usage marker, MAC-RESOURCE addresses and channel allocations, names of LLC / MLE / MM / CMCE messages, SDS text / status / positions (Messages, Subscribers, map), speech of traffic slots (see [TETRA](#tetra))
- **DMR** (Tier II / III, repeater, mobile or direct mode): 4FSK 4800 Bd → colour code, time slots, voice calls (from → to, emergency, encrypted, talker alias), CSBK, data and SMS, raw AMBE+2 frames (see [DMR](#dmr))
- **Analog video** (FPV 5.8 GHz, broadcast and satellite TV) from raw SDR IQ: FM / AM demodulator → composite video → line and field sync, PAL / NTSC, colour, interlace → picture (see [Analog video](#analog-video))
- Building blocks: CRC, scrambler, interleaver, convolutional encoder / Viterbi, sync word search, async serial, NRZ clock, text ↔ bits

### Music
- Synths (2 osc, 4 voices), acid bass (303), drum sequencer, Files › audio (sample library) with an audio editor (see [Sample editor](#sample-editor))
- **Tracker**: plays and edits MOD (ProTracker), S3M (Scream Tracker 3) and XM (FastTracker 2); split into graph nodes: song, single channels, instruments played by your own generator modules, pattern view, and every editor panel as its own node — lay them out in dashboard tiles as your own Renoise-style editor (see [Tracker](#tracker))
- **Sampler**: polyphonic (4 voices) player of clips from Files › audio, pitched by `freq` — plays from the tracker, piano roll or MIDI
- Piano roll, step sequencer, generative melody, arrangement playlist, master clock
- MIDI keyboard input, ADSR envelope
- **Winamp** (Music; no skins): **Music Player (Winamp)** plays one track — play / pause / stop, seek, volume, balance, speed, elapsed / remaining time, kbps / kHz / mono / stereo, stereo `l` / `r` outputs plus `pos`, `time`, `playing`, `end`, `title`; keys **Z X C V B** while it is focused; the next track is decoded in advance, so tracks follow without a gap. The track list is a separate, reusable node **Playlist** (`modules/playlist.js`): it keeps the list in the patch, with shuffle / repeat off / all / one and drag-to-reorder, and wires to a player — `fs` / `url` / `name` / `go` / `nfs` / `nurl` go to the player, `next` / `prev` / `end` / `req` / `playing` come back. Files live in the shared file storage (the same as **Files** and the Sample Library): **files** puts a copy into *Files → Music* (or into the library as mono clips — the *→ Samples* choice), **library** picks from the file manager, files can be dragged from a Files node onto the playlist or sent by wire (`add` ← Files `send`). URLs and **Internet Radio** stations (`url` + `name` wires, or all found stations from Internet Radio's `stations` output (every station, with or without coordinates) into `rows` with the **stations** button) play as streams through `<audio>`, like Audio Stream. **Media Library** (`modules/medialib.js`, logic in `modules/medialib-kernels.js`, `node tools/test-medialib.mjs`) is the tree of the classic Winamp library: *Local files* (Music — the *Music* folder of Files, Recently added, Artists, Albums, from the ID3 tags) and *Network services → Internet Radio* (stations from the `rows` wire). A click on a category puts its tracks on the `list` output, a double click also pulses `play`; wire `list` → Playlist `set` (replaces the list) and `play` → Playlist `req`. **Graphic Equalizer (10 bands)** (Audio) — Winamp's bands 60 Hz … 16 kHz, preamp, 16 presets, response curve, double click a slider to zero it. Presets *Winamp* (library + player + playlist + equalizer + Radial Spectrum / Spectrum Bars; the ▦ view lays them out like the classic Winamp) and *Winamp: Internet Radio Playlist*. Logic: `modules/player-kernels.js`, `node tools/test-player.mjs`
- **Band FFT (few bins)** (Processing): an FFT that outputs only 4…128 bands (log / mel / linear scale, tilt dB/oct, fall time) as a `spec`; also `bass` / `mid` / `treble` / `level` (0…1), a `beat` pulse and the tempo `bpm` (median of the beat intervals)
- **Visualizers**: **Spectrum Bars** (Analysis; styles bars / round / led / mirror / line, peak markers; takes Band FFT or a plain FFT and groups it into bars itself) and, in Music, **Radial Spectrum** (rotating circle, bass pulse, flash on the beat), **Neon Waveform** (triggered line, ring, XY / Lissajous), **Beat Particles** (burst on the beat, fountain by bands, warp flight) and **Ridgeline Spectrum** (the "Unknown Pleasures" 3D history). Presets in *Music: Visualizers*. Logic: `modules/viz-kernels.js`, `node tools/test-viz.mjs`
  - Ridgeline **rf mode** is a 3D waterfall for the air: the whole source span with absolute frequencies, the maximum of the bins per column (narrow carriers are kept), a frequency axis and dB legend, `fpeak` output and the peak in the readout — feed it an FFT / spectrum from a USB SDR or tinySA (preset *Ridgeline Spectrum: Air History (RF mode)*)

### Output & extensibility
- Sound card output (per-node output device selection, peak/clip meter), WAV / MP3 recording (files named by date and time; optionally streamed straight to a disk file, so the length is not limited by memory — Chrome/Edge), log to a [Table](#table), trigger recorder (modes manual / **gate** — records while `trig` is high, e.g. the receiver's `sqOpen`, plus *post-record* time after it closes / level / edge; *tag* goes into the clip name)
- Sound card output (per-node output device selection, peak/clip meter), WAV / MP3 recording (files named by date and time), log to a [Table](#table), trigger recorder
- **Network Out**: WebSocket (`ws://`, `wss://`, reconnect) — text on change, numbers as a JSON object every period, or mono PCM16 audio (a JSON header first, then binary blocks). Example: `websocat -s 8765` on the receiving side
- **Map** (offline vector base map, OSM / satellite / relief tiles and overlays, GeoJSON / KML / GPX layers, tracks, markers — see [Map and records](#map-and-records)), screen transmitter, indicators
- **Beep** (Output): a `go` pulse plays a short tone through its own audio context (independent of the engine; the browser allows sound only after a tap on the page). Pitch (the `freq` input overrides), length, number of tones in a row and the pause between them, volume, wave, *min gap between beeps*; **Test** plays one. Used in the preset *Object Detect: Camera Video and Photo* (a beep on every new object).
- **Vibrate** (Output): a `go` pulse vibrates the phone (Vibration API) for *duration* or the `ms` input; *pattern* (`200 100 200`, ms) makes a series. Android Chrome / Firefox only, the page needs a tap first
- **Notify**: speaks a text aloud (speech synthesis), shows a system notification and/or vibrates — on a new text or a `go` pulse, with a minimum gap between alerts (e.g. a decoded message or a CFAR detection → voice alert)
- **MQTT Out**: publish text or a number (template `{v}` / `{v:N}`) on change; topic from a parameter or a wire, QoS 0/1, retain. Example — an IR blaster on Tasmota: *IR Encode* (output format «tasmota mqtt») → MQTT Out `cmnd/<device>/IRsend`
- **Serial Out (WebSerial)**: write text or a numeric value to a serial port — Arduino, relays, transceiver CAT control (template `FA{v:11};` turns a frequency into a Kenwood command); device replies come back on the `reply` output
- **HTTP Out**: webhook request (POST / PUT / GET, custom headers) — text on change, or numbers as a JSON object on a `go` pulse or every period. The server must allow CORS, and from the https demo only `https://` (or localhost) works
- **MIDI Out** (Web MIDI): `gate` → note on/off, `note` (or `freq` in Hz straight from *MIDI Keyboard*) with velocity, and a `cc` input for control change — drive hardware synths and a DAW from any signal
- **Firmware flashers** (WebSerial, Chrome/Edge): **Flasher: Quansheng UV-K5** **Flasher: Radtel RT-880** and **Flasher: Radtel RT-890** and **Flasher: Radtel RT-4D** — see [Firmware flashers](#firmware-flashers)
- Module Builder and Script nodes for writing custom DSP code in the browser

## USB SDR

The **USB SDR** node talks to the receiver directly over WebUSB (Chrome, Edge, Opera), no drivers or native software needed. Up to 4 demodulator channels share one device.

Modes: WFM (stereo and RDS on request, see below), NFM, AM, SAM, USB, LSB, raw IQ. **SAM** (synchronous AM) locks a PLL onto the carrier and detects coherently: less distortion during selective fading, and **SAM sideband** = USB/LSB keeps only one sideband to dodge interference on the other. In AM/SAM/SSB the tuner is always parked sr/4 away from the channel, so the DC spike never lands on the carrier.

For HF: **noise blanker** cuts short impulses (power-line, switching supplies) on the raw wideband IQ before the channel filter, where a pulse is still short; **auto notch** (NLMS predictor) removes steady whistles/heterodynes from AM/SAM/SSB audio; **SAM sideband = ISB** puts the upper sideband in the left channel and the lower in the right, so you hear which side the interference is on.

| Device | Sample rate | Samples | Linux kernel modules to unload |
|---|---|---|---|
| RTL-SDR (RTL2832U + R820T/R828D, incl. Blog V4) | up to 3.2 MSPS | 8 bit | `dvb_usb_rtl28xxu` |
| HackRF One / Jawbreaker / rad1o | 2–20 MSPS | 8 bit | `hackrf` |
| Airspy R2 / Mini | rates reported by firmware | 12 bit | `airspy` |
| HydraSDR RFOne (38af:0001) | rates reported by firmware | 12 bit and up, per rate | `airspy` |
| Airspy HF+ (Dual / Discovery / Ranger) | rates reported by firmware (768 kSPS typical) | 16 bit | — |
| SDRplay RSP1 and clones, MSi2500 + MSi001 TV sticks | 1.3–15 MSPS | 14 bit up to 6 MSPS, then 12 / 10 / 8 bit | `msi001`, `msi2500` |
| RX-888 mkI / mkII / mkIII (SDDC, Cypress FX3) | 0.25–10 MSPS out of a 16-bit ADC at up to 66 MSPS | 16 bit ADC, DDC in the browser | — |

Common controls: gain (auto or manual), bias-tee, ppm correction, center shift off DC. Manual gain works only with *auto* off — moving the gain slider (or wiring its input) switches *auto* off by itself. On R820T / R828D (incl. RTL-SDR Blog V4) the LNA and mixer steps follow librtlsdr's table exactly (29 steps, 0–49.6 dB, VGA 16.3 dB); the slider rounds up to the next step.

**ADC overload**: the status line shows the ADC peak and mean power in dBFS over the last ~0.5 s, and **⚠ OVERLOAD** (held for 3 s) when more than 0.01% of I/Q samples sit on the ADC rails. With an 8-bit RTL-SDR a strong local station easily does that, and the spectrum then fills with intermod products that are not real signals — lower the gain until the warning goes away; a mean level around −30…−15 dBFS is usually a good spot. Outputs `adcPk`, `adcRms` (dBFS), `clip` (% of samples) and `ovl` (0/1) let a patch react, e.g. gate a detector or step the gain down.

**WFM: stereo and RDS.** Both are off the CPU until you ask for them: *WFM stereo* is **off** by default (mono is enough for most listening and saves the 19 kHz pilot PLL and the L−R filter), and the RDS decoder runs **only while something is wired to the `rds` output** (or to `ps` / `rt`, which still work as before). `rds` is a `rec` output: one record per change of what has been received (at most four a second) — `{t, src:'RDS', kind:'rds', id, freq, pi, call, ps, rt, pty, ptyName, tp, ta, ms, country, bler, snap}`, where `snap` is the full decoded state. Wire it into **RDS Display**, or into a Table / Rec Log for a log of stations. Retuning clears the state.

**RDS Display** (Radio) draws what has been decoded: the station name (PS), PI with the US call sign (RBDS: K/W stations) and the coverage area, the country (from ECC and PI; only ECC E0…E3 are in the table), the share of good blocks, the flags **TP, TA, music / speech and DI** (stereo, artificial head, compressed, dynamic PTY; dashed = not received yet), RadioText, **RT+** (artist, title and other tags, `♪` line), programme type (RDS or RBDS names) and PTYN, the station clock with its UTC offset, the programme item number (PIN), ECC / language code, **alternative frequencies** (FM, LF / MF; the tuned one is lit), applications announced in group 3A (RT+, TMC, …), other networks (**EON**: PS, PTY, TA, AF) and a bar chart of the groups received (0A … 15B). Records of several stations (a scanner) become chips at the bottom: click one to pin it. Outputs: `ps`, `rt`, `artist`, `title`, `pty`, `sync`. The layout follows the size of the node or the dashboard tile: from one line with the name and the radiotext in a narrow strip to five columns of cards with all lists on a wide tile.

| RDS Display | Dark | Light |
|---|---|---|
| Desktop — wide tile | <img src="docs/screenshots/rds-wide-dark.png" alt="RDS Display, wide tile, dark"> | <img src="docs/screenshots/rds-wide-light.png" alt="RDS Display, wide tile, light"> |
| Desktop — tile next to a spectrum | <img src="docs/screenshots/rds-dash-dark.png" alt="RDS Display next to a spectrum, dark"> | <img src="docs/screenshots/rds-dash-light.png" alt="RDS Display next to a spectrum, light"> |
| Low strip under a spectrum | <img src="docs/screenshots/rds-strip-dark.png" alt="RDS Display, low strip, dark"> | <img src="docs/screenshots/rds-strip-light.png" alt="RDS Display, low strip, light"> |
| Phone — narrow tile | <img width="260" src="docs/screenshots/rds-phone-dark.png" alt="RDS Display, phone, dark"> | <img width="260" src="docs/screenshots/rds-phone-light.png" alt="RDS Display, phone, light"> |

The screenshots were made on a synthetic test station (with RT+, AF and EON), not on a real broadcast.

*Not checked:* RT+ (group layout and tag positions), the country table (only ECC E0…E3) and the decoding of method B alternative frequencies follow the standard from memory and were verified only against the built-in test encoder (`node tools/test-rds.mjs`), not against real captures. The *IQ Demodulator* block has its own, simpler RDS (name and radiotext only).
